Title: Annales regum Iyāsu II et Iyo'as; interpretatus est Ignatius Guidi (Corpus Scriptorum Christianorum Orientalium, Scriptores Aethiopici, Versio, series altera, tomus VI)
Creator: Anonymous royal chronicles (the title page names no author); translated into French by Ignazio Guidi (title page: 'interpretatus est Ignatius Guidi')
Publisher: Romae: excudebat Karolus de Luigi; Parisiis: Carolus Poussielgue; Lipsiae: Otto Harrassowitz (title page)
Publication Date: 1912 (title page: 'MDCCCCXII', read on the page image, leaf n8; the archive.org record's date field reads 1910)
Rights: Public Domain (pd-us-by-date: published 1912); the archive.org record carries no rights field, so the imprint date is the basis
Language: fra
Source: archive.org item annalesregumiysu01guid, full text from the item's own djvu.txt. Fetched directly 2026-11-15 by the Library thread; the byte count of the downloaded file (633,538) equals the size archive.org lists for the djvu.txt.
Provenance note: Extracted unaltered: the item's own djvu.txt is reproduced whole after this header; nothing was stripped, normalized or corrected. Rights basis: published 1912, out of US copyright by date. OCR quality: mostly clean French with regular errors (q read as d: 'dui' for 'qui'); Ethiopic words in the notes are garbled. Cover-scan noise stands at OCR lines 1-160. Word count of the host text: 113,770. Line numbers cited in this header are lines of the host OCR; add 12 for this file's own line numbers.
Content note: The translation is in French; only the editor's note on the manuscripts (OCR lines 322-364) is in Latin. The history of Iyasu II (Adyam Sagad) and the queen Berhan Mogasa from the heading at 393, chapter I at 897, to the king's burial at about 10555. The history of Iyo'as from 10557 to about 15790. Guidi's 'Observation' at 15792 and additions to tome V at 15802-15825. Footnotes run throughout; there is no index.

Au nom le Dieu trine dans les personnes et un dans la divi- 
nite; etre primordial sans avoir de hier, etre final sans avoir 
de demain, dui du neant a cree les anges et broluit les mon- 
des. Son existence na bas le cause ni son etre dle principe, 
mais lui-meme est la cause le toute creature. ll est le fon- 
dement et 1 accomplissement, il est le commencement et la 
fin. ll a suspendu le ciel comme une vonte, et a aifermi la 
terre sur les eaux. Personne ne 1a aide par sa force ou sa

fonde les angles de la terre, personne n’etait avec lui dui 
tint le cordeau 1. Meme les anges netaient bas avec lui pour 
1 aider, mais ils furent creés avec elle. () cluel livre admi-

l'enfantement du Fils dui naduit d'une naissance ineilable, et

la valeur de ces titres Voir les Observations a 1a fin des tomes IV et NV.
